5.1 
Dataset 
SWaT dataset is used for intrusion detection system. 936,722 samples totaling 51 
characteristics were gathered over the course of 11 days and are included in the 
dataset. SWaT features 26 actuators and 25 sensors. There are 53 distinct values in 
the SWaT dataset. One of them is for the timestamp, one is for the label (“Attack” or 
“Normal”), and the remaining 51 are the recorded data of 51 sensors and actuators. 
The attacks on SWaT system are divided into four types based on attack points in 
each stage 
1. Single Stage Single Point (SSSP): It is performed on one speciﬁc attack point in 
one stage. 
2. Single Stage Multi-point (SSMP): It is performed on multiple attack points in a 
single stage. 
3. Multi-stage Single Point (MSSP): It is performed on multiple stages with a max-
imum of one attack point. 
4. Multi-stage Multi-point (MSMP): It is performed on two or more attack points 
of multiple stages. 
In 936,722 samples, there were 496,801 Normal data and 496,801 Attack data 
in respective Normal and Attack csv. The given attack dataset was not sufﬁcient for 
multi-classiﬁcation as the type of attack was not mentioned in the dataset. 
To identify which type of attack, created a Python program to label the type of 
attacks that happened in each row. In P1 stage, the operating range of FIT101 is 
between 2.5 and 2.6 and LIT101 is between 500 and 1100. Like this each sensor’s 
operating range is given in the paper [ 11]. With the help of the operating range given 
in the table of the paper [ 11], the attack is identiﬁed.

attacks only. That makes the dataset imbalanced. So we created other three attack

TITLE:   Detecting Financial Fraud: A Comparative Study of Quantum-Based Supervised Learning Algorithms
AUTHORS: ['Farooq Khattak', 'Yap Thiam Hoe', 'Nusaibah Khalid Abdulmajeed', 'Charles Lim', 'Hager Basil Mohammed Salh', 'Muhammad Khalid']
YEAR:    2025   VENUE: Proceedings of Sixth Doctoral Symposium on Computational Intelligence (DoSCI-2025), Lecture Notes in Networks and Systems
DOI:        ARXIV: None
TAGS:    ['fraud-detection', 'quantum-ml-finance']
-- ABSTRACT --
This empirical study compares quantum-enhanced supervised learning approaches for financial fraud detection, evaluating four Quantum Graph Neural Network variants: VQC, QSVC, EQNN, and SQNN. The results show QSVC achieving the best balance of accuracy and precision (accuracy 91.3%, precision 92.6%, recall 86.1%, F1 89.2%), indicating its suitability for practical fraud detection while noting the need for further optimization and scalability work. This empirical study reports the demonstration of 'quantum supremacy' using a 53-qubit superconducting processor (Sycamore) to perform a speciﬁc sampling task that the authors argue would be intractable for classical supercomputers. The experiment s
-- METHOD --
The study implements and compares several quantum-enhanced supervised learning approaches for credit-card fraud detection using a quantum-graph-neural-network (QGNN) style pipeline. Classical preprocessing and feature-compression are applied to transaction records to reduce dimensionality and to produce concise node feature vectors. The compacted features are quantum-encoded (angle/rotation encoding) into a quantum Hilbert space and processed by parameterized variational quantum circuits (VQCs). Several QGNN variants are evaluated: Variational Quantum Classifier (VQC), Quantum Support Vector Classifier (QSVC), Estimator Quantum Neural Network (EQNN), and Sampler Quantum Neural Network (SQNN)

6 
Advancing Efﬁciency with Data Lineage 
Data lineage redeﬁnes program management effectiveness because it gives detailed 
insight into data movement among projects [11]. Data lineage ensures data integrity 
and quality between programs through source tracking and data transformation [14]. 
Data lineage aids users to identify and ﬁx data deﬁnition or formatting issues and 
wrong inputs and calculations and missing and incomplete data and prevent unautho-
rized changes or tampering13 . A data-centric approach improves decision-making 
while ensuring transparency and promoting collaboration among stakeholders. 
7 
Beneﬁts of Data Lineage for Managers 
Program management derives many advantages to managers when data lineage 
integration is adopted. All of the stakeholders can clearly observe the decision-
making process and associated data that fosters conﬁdence and accountability. Teams 
feel greater collaboration when they understand data interdependencies since it 
promotes effective communication and coordination among team members. Data 
ﬂow tracing also facilitates proactive risk avoidance: Program managers avoid 
incorrect executions by identifying risks in advance and taking prompt actions 
(Table 1). 
8 
Enhancing Insights Using AI 
8.1 
AI-Driven Analytics in Program Management 
Program managers leveraging AI-powered analytics acquire unique capabilities to 
optimize program effectiveness and efﬁciency. AI applies predictive analytics to 
maximize the use of resources by forecasting project requirements and demand that 
maximizes resource utilization. AI models that have the capability to forecast time-
lines can identify potential project delays and the reason for the delay and suggest


Advancing Program Management Efﬁciency Using Data Lineage …
7
corrective action to restore the momentum of a project. Managers are able to study 
stakeholder feedback with the help of NLP that allows them to shape their commu-
nication channels to suit changing needs and issues leading to increased stakeholder 
participation and satisfaction. 
8.2 
Integration with Program Management Tools 
Program management software such as JIRA and Monday.com can now be equipped 
with the ability to integrate AI modules with superior analytics into existing work-
ﬂows. Businesses can empower their project managers to make informed decisions 
by making superior analytics easily accessible through the simple integration of AI 
modules into regular workﬂows without the necessity of complex setups or technical 
knowledge. 
8.3 
Combined Power: Data Lineage and AI 
Together, when combined, data lineage and AI systems create powerful synergies that 
result in enhanced program management effectiveness. Data lineage provides orga-
nizations with end-to-end visibility of data movement and information data sources 
along with transformation histories and interrelationships between various sets of 
data. AI models achieve greater precision and reliability through extended histor-
ical data backgrounds for processing as identiﬁed by [15]. AI systems can auto-
mate complex data lineage mapping which traditionally requires substantial time 
and manual effort. AI-powered lineage mapping tools enable rapid identiﬁcation of 
data dependencies while tracking data provenance and visualizing information ﬂow 
through multiple systems and projects [12]. Program managers can optimize resource 
allocation and mitigate risks to enhance overall program performance by utilizing 
data lineage insights together with AI’s predictive and prescriptive powers for better 
decision-making [10]. 
9 
Integrating Data Lineage and AI in Organizations 
Organizations must adopt a thorough integration approach to harness the combined 
strengths of data lineage with AI analytics for program management. 
Assess Organizational Readiness: Examine the data maturity level within an 
organization alongside its technological capabilities and readiness for change. 
1. Choose Appropriate Tools and Platforms: Discover program management plat-
forms that offer smooth integration of data lineage with AI-powered analytics


8
A. Agarwal et al.
capabilities through solutions like JIRA and Monday.com or equivalent enterprise 
platforms. 
2. Establish Data Governance and Security Protocols: Maintain data quality and 
security through the implementation of strong data governance structures. 
3. Train Teams on Effective Utilization: Project managers and stakeholders need 
complete training and support to understand how to use data lineage combined 
with AI analytics effectively in program management. 
4. Continuously Monitor and Optimize: Perform regular assessments of how well 
the integrated data lineage and AI-driven analytics solution operates and delivers 
results. 
By integrating data lineage and AI-powered analytics into program management 
practices organizations streamline operations and help project teams make better-
informed decisions that lead to higher operational efﬁciency and success. 
9.1 
Challenges and Limitations 
1. Technical Challenges: Creating solid data lineage and AI systems involves signif-
icant technical complexity during implementation. Implementing robust tech 
solutions becomes particularly challenging for organizations with existing legacy 
infrastructure. Organizations can manage the technical challenges by using 
phasing and cloud-based tools which will gradually bring necessary capabilities 
[6]. 
2. Organizational Resistance: Change management is necessary to help teams tran-
sition from traditional workﬂows to new systems. The beneﬁts of the new work-
ﬂow are easy to communicate, and its introduction can proceed step-by-step. 
Stakeholders will be more likely to support the initiative when they receive 
practical training. 
3. Cost and Resource Constraints: Organizations face substantial initial costs in 
acquiring technology and training their workforce. Organizations need to evaluate 
the upfront costs against long-term advantages that stem from better efﬁciency 
paired with lower risks and superior decision-making capabilities thanks to the 
combination of integrated data lineage with AI-powered insights [4]. 
Organizations that proactively resolve these issues and limitations unlock the full 
potential of data lineage and AI to achieve program management excellence which 
drives sustainable organizational growth.


Advancing Program Management Efﬁciency Using Data Lineage …
9
10 
Future Directions 
10.1 
Emerging Technologies 
The latest developments like blockchain technology for secure data lineage and 
quantum computing’s ability to boost AI capabilities present substantial opportunities 
to improve program management efﬁciency and effectiveness [7]. 
10.2 
Expanding Scope 
This paper examines data lineage and AI-driven analytics applications in program 
management, but these transformative technologies can lead to innovations and oper-
ational improvements across multiple sectors such as healthcare and construction as 
well as manufacturing and others. 
10.3 
Research Opportunities 
The ongoing evolution of program management together with fast-developing data 
lineage and AI opens up many opportunities for future research and exploration. 
Future research should explore AI decision-making ethics including bias and trans-
parency while assessing data lineage scalability for global enterprises and through 
cross-industry case studies validate the paper’s theoretical models and practical 
technology applications across different organizations. 
11 
Conclusion 
Managers will experience a comprehensive transformation in handling inefﬁcien-
cies when they utilize data lineage together with AI insights. The combination 
of enhanced transparency with predictive analysis and actionable insights leads 
to improved decision-making which produces real results. While initial difﬁculties 
present themselves, the substantial beneﬁts exceeding these barriers require careful 
consideration by managers. The combination of data integration with AI insights and 
governance procedures alongside team support and ongoing optimization within a 
uniﬁed structure unleashes the complete potential of these technologies to revolu-
tionize program management operations. The program management ﬁeld will trans-
form through seamless integration of emerging leading-edge technologies such as 
blockchain and quantum computing.

Result Analysis 
The graph shown in Fig. 3 displays a federated learning model’s accuracy percentages 
over the course of six rounds (r1 through r6). Multiple coloured bars are used to 
indicate the data for each round, which might be various model versions, training 
sets, or centres that are involved in the federated learning process. In round 1 (r1), 
starting point accuracy lies in the 70–85% range. The distribution of values shows 
that performance varies throughout situations or versions. In round 2 (r2), with most 
results falling within 75–88%, accuracy increases. This advises that the model be 
improved or that data input from different centres be more uniform. In round 3 (r3), 
there is still room for improvement, with accuracy mainly falling between 78 and 
90%.

An Intelligent Federated Analytics Model for Multi-centre Imaging …
19
The two main methods of machine learning are federated learning (FL) and 
centralised models. Although each has advantages, they serve various purposes 
and have different strengths in centralised models. Data collection necessitates the 
centralization of all data in one place and training using the complete dataset, a 
model is trained by a central server. Beneﬁts effective training: Able to make use of 
strong hardware and reﬁned algorithms. Simple deployment: Clients can immediately 
receive the trained model. Drawbacks: Privacy hazards arise from the centralization 
of sensitive data. Costs associated with data transfer: Relocating sizable datasets can 
be costly and time-consuming. Single point of failure: One crucial point of failure 
is the central server. Data distribution in federated learning (FL) is decentralised, 
with each client having their own copy. Training: Using their own data, clients train 
local models, which are then combined to form a global model. Beneﬁts privacy 
preservation: Sensitive information is shielded by not sharing raw data. 
The graph shown in Fig. 4 displays the resource utilisation% for each of the six 
rounds (r1 through r6). Around 42% of resources are used, which indicates a modest 
use of resources at ﬁrst. The use of resources has somewhat decreased to around 40%. 
This might indicate that there was less work done this round or some optimisation. A 
further decline is noted, resulting in a utilisation of around 37%. This might suggest 
a decrease in demand at this time or more efﬁciency in the use of resources. Similar 
to round 3, the resource utilisation stays constant at around 37%. This stability points 
to a steady use of resources. There is a discernible rise to the highest point in the 
recorded rounds, which is about 46%. This rise can be the result of inefﬁcient resource 
allocation during this round or increased demand. The application. The utilisation 
declines little to around 43%, which is less
-- RESULTS WINDOW 3 [77655:80355] --
achieving up to 95% accuracy at the conclusion of the testing procedure. This illus-
trates FL’s capacity to effectively handle scattered data for CVD diagnosis across a 
range of clinical datasets. 
5 
Conclusion 
This study examined the possibility of federated learning (FL) in building illness diag-
nostic models in a decentralised healthcare system, taking privacy considerations into 
consideration. The goal was to use cardiovascular magnetic resonance (CMR) data to 
diagnose hypertrophic cardiomyopathy (HCM) across several centres. Due to limi-
tations in geographically dispersed healthcare data and data privacy laws, the study 
highlighted the drawbacks of traditional centralised machine learning techniques. 
Federated learning was presented as a workable method that enables collaboration 
on model development without sharing raw data. Using a 3D CNN model that was 
adapted from motion recognition, it proposes a federated learning architecture that 
integrates spatial information. They discussed pre-processing techniques and data 
augmentation tactics to improve model performance. The limitations of standard 
centralised machine learning were also covered in the article, taking into account data 
privacy regulations and the challenges of retrieving medical records from geographi-
cally dispersed locations. An intriguing prospect: Federated learning was presented as 
a potential remedy that would enable collaboration on model construction without 
requiring the exchange of raw data. Despite the enormous improvements demon-
strated by the suggested AI-driven federated learning (FL) model for multi-centre 
imaging diagnosis in cardiovascular sickness, certain limitations need to be addressed 
in future study.

Evaluation metrics for performance as well as comparison are highly dependent on

of ground truth training sets, and the issue of class imbalance in medical images. The 
evaluation concerns of particular interest in brain tumor segmentation is as follows: 
accurate methods will show the number of imaging samples evaluated relative to

Despite the difﬁculties, the emergence of assessment and comparison in the 
new ﬁeld is reported as necessary by the ofﬁcials to be capable of conﬁguring and 
improving such algorithms in execution in a clinical context. Automated brain tumor 
segmentation is employed for clinical usage in a variety of contexts, including treat-
ment planning, image-guided surgery, and the long-term monitoring and analysis of 
changes to the tumor. As for the trends in the algorithmic approach to the problem, the 
specialists report that the area is marked by the emergence of special-purpose neural 
networks trained speciﬁcally for particular subtypes of brain tumor, as well as the 
use of new approaches to image fusion and multimodal imaging [16]. This approach 
presupposes incorporating multiple imaging modalities into a single processing ﬂow 
for optimal effect. In the long term, the specialists may attempt to deal with a variety 
of ﬂaws deteriorating the usability of the automated brain tumor segmentation algo-
rithms, such as their sensitivity to a wide range of imaging artifacts, as well as 
disorders and the substantial amount of variability between patients [17]. 
3 
Methodology 
The objective of the research is to improve the existing approach to the segmentation 
of brain tumors using BWT and ML. In immediate sequence, the steps, which were 
to be included, are: data collecting, data preprocessing, segmentation of features, 
accuracy estimation and ﬁnal prediction. At this point, for the ﬁrst section of the 
methodology, we accessed the diverse data on brain tumor from both CT scan and 
glioma MRI. The images were collected from many patients, and their overall amount 
was 4320 in total. In such a way, various types of brain and tumor, which can be of 
different to size and position, were included in the dataset. Thus, the wide dataset is 
crucial for proper training of the model, which entails the model can see different 
type and get readiness for miscellaneous situations in clinical practice. The proposed 
research design is clear on Fig. 1.
The collected images were preprocessed. Namely, their quality was enhanced, 
and the artifacts, noise, and other disturbing components were reduced. The standard 
methods of preprocessing such as noise reduction, intensity normalization, and image 
registration were applied to the images. The reason for the preprocessing was to mak
-- RESULTS WINDOW 5 [98609:101309] --
split ensured that the models 
were trained with respect to a large part of the data, which was stored separately 
for the evaluation. In this way, the overﬁtting was avoided, and the generalization 
performance was unbiased. 
3.1 
Preprocessing of Dataset 
Preprocessing is a critical aspect of medical image analysis in general and brain 
tumor segmentation in particular. The quality of the input images directly inﬂuences 
the quality and reliability of their processing in subsequent steps. Accordingly, in the 
present research, a series of preprocessing techniques have been used to enhance the


30
S. Gupta et al.
quality of images of brain tumors that were collected from CT and MRI scans. One 
of the initially employed steps in this research is noise reduction. The majority of 
types of noise that can affect medical images are gradient-type, and they can emerge 
from a variety of sources from the origin of such images, such as the equipment with 
which they were acquired and physiological sources. In such situations, the use of 
techniques such as Gaussian or median ﬁltering is appropriate and can be effectively 
used to this end, serving to make the image smoother while retaining its relative 
structure. In such a way, the effects of noise were minimized, and the structures of 
the images were clariﬁed; the latter effect was critical as a large part of the noise that 
affects medical images is spatially related to the underlying structures. 
Another critical piece of preprocessing was represented by intensity normaliza-
tion. The acquisition of medical images is complex and varied, as different acquisition 
parameters and scanner types, as well as the speciﬁc tissues examined, almost always 
lead to differences in intensity, among other aspects. Accordingly, techniques such as 
histogram equalization or z-score normalization were used to scale the pixel values 
of images on a single range, making the intensity information of the images directly 
comparable and uniformly processed. The outcome of this step was that the contrast 
of the images was directly improved, making the images more classiﬁable in terms 
of speciﬁc regions and more directly usable for the next steps of processing. Object 
registration is another crucial preprocessing step that was used, as different images 
may be obtained from different temporal points or orientations. In clinical situations, 
images of brain tumors will be examined at multiple times, for example, to monitor 
the progress of a disease or the effectiveness of a speciﬁc treatment. 
3.2 
Feature Extraction 
The Berkeley Wavelet Transformation is one of the main techniques for extraction 
of different features from
-- RESULTS WINDOW 6 [105628:109005] --
 to adjust 
the hierarchical pattern representations from input data. Several convolutional and 
pooling layers were used to capture the spatial hierarchy and local alignment. In


32
S. Gupta et al.
conclusion, CNNs were used judiciously for the purpose of brain tumor segmentation 
due to their ability to learn representative and relevant features directly from the input 
image without the need for manual extraction. Meanwhile, feature vector extracted 
at different levels are considered in other machine learning models, but CNNs used 
them as input without any manual processing, which is another advantage. VGG 16 
is another model, which is deep and considered more difﬁcult in comparison with 
more recent architectures. However, it consists of a few convolutional layers with a 
smaller receptive ﬁeld and is followed by a max-pooling layer to reduce sampling. In 
conclusion, the ﬁne-tuning of VGG 16 model with extracted features of brain tumors 
allows them to be used for improved segmentation performance. 
4 
After training and testing different models, the results showed various levels of 
accuracy in predicting tumor regions on brain images. The modiﬁed Support Vector 
Machine showed the highest accuracy as it had a remarkable accuracy rate of 98.65% 
on the classiﬁcation of the tumor and non-tumor region. The high accuracy is because 
the SVM had been modiﬁed to serve the speciﬁc purpose of the medical images 
analysis. The VGG 16 followed with an accuracy of 95.45%, which is still effective 
as the model was able to learn discriminative features directly from the raw images 
data. Additionally, the VGG 16 was simple and could function effectively despite it 
not being complex like other. 
The CNN and RNN followed with an accuracy rate of 93.4% and 91.2%, respec-
tively. CNNs, which are best for capturing spatial hierarchies and local patterns, had 
slightly higher accuracy. However, the ability of RNNs to capture temporal depen-
dencies may have been able to provide information on the sequence pattern on the 
regions of the tumor. Nevertheless, the slightly lower accuracy compared to the modi-
ﬁed SVM and VGG 16 may suggest improvements in their architectures or training 
methods. The result of the accuracy are shown in Fig. 2.
The performance scores based on the evaluation of each model offer essential 
information on the effectiveness of each model in segmenting brain tumors from 
medical imaging data. The result of analysis are shown in Fig. 2. Our results establish 
that the modiﬁed Support Vector Machine is the top performer with high precision, 
recall, F1 score, and accuracy as seen from their values which are approximately 
98.65%, 98.55%, 98.60% and 98.62%, respectively. This shows that the model can 
accurately classify both the tumor and non-tumor region with the highest conﬁ-
dence levels. These performance indices are indicative of the ability of the model to 
allow clinical users to determine tumor regions on MR images. Presented in Fig. 3, 
the VGG16 model is the next performer with high precision, recall, F1 score, and 
accuracy of approximately 95.55, 95.35, 95.45, and 95.50%.
However, the simplicity of the model compared to more complex architectures has 
been demonstrated in the current study. These overall results prove that the VGG16 
model was able to learn discriminative features through

classiﬁcations.

5 
Conclusion 
Even though the RNN model exhibits slightly elevated false positives and false 
negatives, it still demonstrates acceptable performance in brain tumor segmentation. 
Overall, these results highlight the value of machine learning-based techniques in 
automated brain tumor segmentation, thus enhancing the array of tools available 
for clinical decision-making in neuro-oncology. Nonetheless, further research and 
optimization efforts are required to reﬁne the performance of the models and facilitate 
their application in clinical practice. With continued development, the utilization of 
the discussed models will likely promote superior patient care and outcomes in the 
diagnosis and treatment of brain tumors.

Related Works 
In [20], the authors proposed a multipath routing scheme to improve the street 
and path consistency in VANETs. Packet delivery ratio and end-to-end delay have 
improved using the multipath model. An ant colony optimization algorithm based on 
clustering presents a new relay-selection mechanism. The result mentioned that the
ACO algorithm using clustering improved the delay but failed to decrease the routing
overhead. In [21], the authors developed reliability-based clustering in VANET and 
introduced a diverted path approach to improve V2V communication reliability. 
The notion of a backup link has improved, as has V2I communication reliability.
The results show that DPA has improved in V2V communication, but a delay has
occurred.
In [22], the authors improve the clustering algorithm in VANET. In this technique, 
the road is segmented by grid partitioning before giving information to the cluster 
center. All clustering task, that is, cluster head selection, removing, and mer ging, is
based on an integrated approach called center-based evolving clustering based on
grid partitioning. This approach failed to decrease the delay. In [23], the authors 
introduced a mobility-aware dynamic clustering-based routing protocol in IoV to 
improve the lifetime of the network and reduce the delay. This proposed method has 
cluster formation and cluster head selection. Cluster formation based on Euclidean
distance and cluster selection based on the Mayﬂy optimization algorithm but failed
to improve the network lifetime.
In [24], the authors mentioned safety in VANET. In the proposed mechanism, 
the whale optimization-based clustering algorithm minimizes the clusters before 
increasing the number of communication nodes. T his approach is used to reduce
the number of clusters and delay but also to reduce energy consumption. In [25], 
the authors mentioned clustering stability in VANET by use of a spectral clus-
tering algorithm. This technique is the combination of spectral clustering, a modiﬁed


40
A. S. Malhan et al.
force-directed algorithm, and a whole optimization technique to improve the c luster
stability and lifetime network. In [26], the authors proposed an optimum transmission 
probability in cluster-based medium access control protocol to improve
